# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

## User Profile

Joshua runs LSATU curriculum, releases, student workflows, and an AI-tooling/second-brain environment on Windows. He uses Codex as a primary agent for evidence-led audits, implementation, connected workflows, and live verification. Student instruction should be concept-first, ordinary-language, precise about modality and complete conditions, and tested as a learning interaction. He is configuring Orca as a multi-agent workspace with Codex primary.

## User preferences

- Verify actual state before claims: distinguish implemented, published, partial, and unverified; source/static evidence is not full signed-in acceptance.
- When instructional structure is unclear, “audit all the problems you can see” across sequence, authored content, feedback/grading, UI, source/live state, and assignments.
- Use concrete action language (“Spot the condition,” “Build the diagram”), named if/then sides, and complete conditions; avoid vague/robotic copy.
- Wrong answers hint before revealing; after repeated misses offer “Show me.” Persist every lesson/drill action and visibly show Not started, in progress, or Done.
- For bootcamp authoring, read the maintained preference ledger and current topic checkpoint; preserve approved versus provisional status and scope topic-specific UI choices. [ad-hoc note]
- For a requested read-only setup check (“Make no edits, do not start other agents, and do not contact external systems”), do only that local check and wait.
- Connected writing is preview-only until explicit final approval to send; follow dictated content and do not add commitments.
- Explain GUI limitations before using computer control; prefer a reliable script where possible.

## General Tips

- Read C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\AGENTS.md before AI OS work: wiki/hot.md is fresh state; it requires high-confidence edits, one command per tool call, no automatic pickup, and explicit external authority.
- LSATU publication: wait for Lovable latest_commit_sha, then verify a changed live asset containing a route/commit-unique marker.
- Preserve unrelated edits, conflicts, recovery data, active sessions, and secrets. Never retain exposed keys.
- Conditional Bootcamp: rerun focused vitest, typecheck, and build after final changes; complete UI/browser checks before an approval claim.
- Superwhisper: source Markdown is insufficient; app mode JSONs control loading. Run sync.py --restart and read live settings/counts. Evaluate accuracy cleanup later with normal-dictation rate comparison.
- Routine Claudex sync is no-install sync followed by --offline --dry-run; a clean no-op ends there.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: target 34 one-to-one sessions a week
plus consultations (calendar showed ~13/week, 2026-07 to 2026-10). Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

Found the exact Claude chat: **“Spreeder website analysis.”**

The goal is to build **Reading Center** to match Spreeder’s screens and reading behavior, then integrate it into LSAT U.

- **Built:** five reading modes, library management, tags, bookmarks, preferences, speed controls, search, Profile, stats, and goals.
- **Still needs verification:** import, fullscreen, finish alert, themes, shortcuts, visible Flow playback, and remaining Profile behavior against Spreeder.
- **Still needs implementation:** LSAT U integration and its database migration. The LSAT U copy is older than the standalone app.
- **Excluded by prior decision:** AI generation and summaries. Import currently supports text, Markdown, and HTML.

I restarted the app, opened [Reading Center](http://127.0.0.1:8100), and verified **30 passing tests, a clean TypeScript check, and a successful build**. Profile’s main screens also opened correctly.

I recorded the takeover in the [existing spec](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/research/2026-10-13-spreeder-reconstruction-spec.md). The next step is completing standalone browser verification before porting it into LSAT U.

Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Stream water contains animal DNA.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"There are new ways to count wildlife.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 4: The main point is a shift in method or framing, technical vocabulary\\"}, {\\"order\\": 16, \\"prompt\\": \\"The dating of early metalworking sites long leaned on typology: arrange the recovered tools by shape, assume simple forms precede refined ones, and read the sequence as time. The shapes, in other words, stopped telling time and started telling everything else.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Radiometric dating showed that tool shapes do not track time, so archaeologists reassigned typology from dating sites to revealing workshop traditions, trade, and markets, leaving chronology to the lab.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old assumption: simple-to-refined shape sequence equals time · (2) the embarrassment: lab dates showed forms coexisting · (3) the reassignment: shapes now evidence tradition/trade/market, not chronology. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Crude and refined tools were used at the same time.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Archaeologists date metalworking sites.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 4: The main point is a shift in method or framing, technical vocabulary\\"}, {\\"order\\": 17, \\"prompt\\": \\"Courts assessing eyewitness identifications historically asked whether the witness was confident, attentive, and honest, treating memory as a recording whose quality those virtues guaranteed. Decades of controlled experiments reframed the question by showing that identification accuracy depends heavily on conditions no witness controls: lighting, distance, stress, the composition of the lineup, and the feedback given afterward. The experiments do not show that eyewitnesses are generally wrong, and confident witnesses are still right more often than hesitant ones. What the research undermines is the inference from sincerity to accuracy, and courts have slowly begun to instruct juries on viewing conditions rather than character.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Research reframed eyewitness reliability as a matter of viewing and lineup conditions rather than witness character, and while witnesses are not generally wrong, sincerity no longer counts as proof of accuracy.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old frame: confidence and honesty guarantee memory · (2) reframe: accuracy tracks conditions outside the witness\'\'s control · (3) retained concession: witnesses are often right; what falls is the sincerity-to-accuracy inference. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Lineup composition affects identifications.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Eyewitness testimony can be unreliable.\\\\\\" (Overshoots by dropping the concession.)\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 18, \\"prompt\\": \\"Nutrition guidance once ranked dietary fats by their chemistry, condemning saturated varieties wholesale and promoting substitutes with more favorable laboratory profiles. Long-term population studies complicated the ranking by showing that health outcomes track what replaces a discarded fat: swapping it for refined starches yields no benefit, while swapping it for certain plant oils does. The chemistry was not wrong, and some substitutions really are improvements. The revision is in the unit of advice. Foods and their replacements, not molecules in isolation, have become the thing guidance is written about, because a nutrient\'\'s effect proved inseparable from what fills its place at the table.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Nutrition advice shifted from ranking fat molecules to evaluating whole substitutions, because outcomes depend on what replaces a discarded fat, even though the underlying chemistry and some swaps remain valid.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old unit: molecule-level rankings · (2) complication: outcomes track the replacement, not the removal · (3) retained concession: chemistry holds and some substitutions genuinely help; the change is the unit of advice. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Replacing fat with refined starch does not help.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Nutrition science keeps changing its mind.\\\\\\" (The cynical overshoot; the paragraph says the opposite.)\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 19, \\"prompt\\": \\"Flood control on large rivers was for a century an engineering contest: raise the levees, straighten the channel, move the water through faster. Repeated levee failures shifted the field toward a different accounting, in which the cheapest place to put floodwater is often a farm field purchased for occasional inundation rather than a wall built to exclude the water entirely. Engineered defenses still protect cities, and no one proposes removing them. The change is in what counts as infrastructure: acquired floodplain, wetland easements, and setback levees now appear in project budgets alongside concrete, because the failures showed that exclusion alone was purchasing risk at compound interest.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"After repeated levee failures, flood control expanded from pure exclusion engineering to budgeting land that can safely flood as infrastructure, while hard defenses still protect cities.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old contest: higher levees, faster channels · (2) shift: purchased floodplain and setbacks count as infrastructure · (3) retained concession: engineered defenses remain; exclusion alone compounded risk. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Some farm fields are bought to hold floodwater.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Levees sometimes fail.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 20, \\"prompt\\": \\"Histories of literacy long measured it by signatures: count who could sign a parish register, and map reading across a society. Scholars now emphasize that signing was taught after reading and separately from it, so signature counts systematically undercount readers, capturing mostly those schooled long enough to write, and excluding the many taught to read scripture at home who never held a pen. Signature evidence has not been discarded; nothing else survives at comparable scale, and its trends over time remain informative. What changed is the claim it supports: a floor under past literacy rather than an estimate of it.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Because writing was taught after reading, signature counts undercount past readers, so historians now treat them as a floor under literacy rather than an estimate, while still relying on their trends.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) old measure: signatures as literacy · (2) flaw: reading preceded writing, so readers are missed · (3) retained concession: the evidence survives at unmatched scale and still shows trends; its claim is downgraded to a floor. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Many people could read but not sign.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Measuring historical literacy is hard.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 5: Shift plus a retained concession the summary must keep\\"}, {\\"order\\": 21, \\"prompt\\": \\"Early quantitative studies of scientific careers measured productivity by publication counts and found that a small fraction of researchers produced most of the papers, a result read for decades as evidence of rare individual talent. Later work complicated both the measure and the reading. Counting papers weights fields unequally, since publication rates are set by disciplinary custom rather than effort, and the concentration itself proved to be manufactured partly by institutions: early access to equipment, students, and funding compounds, so that small initial differences in opportunity grow into large final differences in output. Talent has not been dismissed as a factor. The revision is subtler: concentration of output is now treated as a joint product of ability and cumulative institutional advantage, and the measure that revealed it is no longer trusted to compare across fields.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"The finding that a few scientists produce most papers is now explained as talent compounded by accumulating institutional advantage rather than rare ability alone, and raw publication counts are no longer trusted across fields.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) original finding and reading: concentrated output proves rare talent · (2) first correction: the measure is field-biased · (3) second correction: institutions compound small early advantages · (4) retained role for talent in a joint explanation. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Some fields publish more papers than others.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"A few scientists write most papers.\\\\\\" (States the finding while missing the reinterpretation, which is the point.)\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 6: Double contrast, abstract subject, maximum load\\"}, {\\"order\\": 22, \\"prompt\\": \\"Classic accounts of trade before state coinage assumed barter, with money arising to solve the awkwardness of matching wants. Records from early cities support a different sequence: temple and palace bookkeepers denominated debts in standard quantities of grain and silver long before coins circulated, and most exchange took the form of credit entries against future harvests. Coinage, when it arrived, served less to enable trade than to let rulers pay soldiers and collect taxes in a medium they controlled. Barter was real, but it flourished mainly between strangers and at frontiers, where no ledger bound the parties. On this account money\'\'s origin is administrative rather than commercial, and the barter story survives as a special case rather than a beginning.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Evidence from early cities suggests money began as administrative accounting for debts, with coins serving state payment and taxation, while barter was a frontier special case rather than the origin of trade.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) classic story: barter first, money as its fix · (2) documented sequence: ledger credit in standard units preceded coins · (3) coinage\'\'s actual role: state payment and taxation · (4) barter retained as a real but special case among strangers. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Bookkeepers recorded debts in grain and silver.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Money has a long history.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 6: Double contrast, abstract subject, maximum load\\"}, {\\"order\\": 23, \\"prompt\\": \\"Sleep research first treated the sleeping brain as idle, then, after recordings revealed intense nocturnal activity, as busy with a single master task, with memory consolidation the leading candidate. Both frames now look too simple. The sleeping brain runs distinct programs in different stages, and interventions that disrupt one stage impair some functions while leaving others intact: slow-wave disruption degrades factual recall but spares motor learning, while dream-stage suppression does something closer to the reverse. Consolidation has not been dethroned as one genuine function among several. What has been abandoned is the search for the purpose of sleep, in favor of mapping which stage serves which function, on the view that sleep is a schedule of maintenance operations rather than a single process with a single point.\\", \\"credited_answers\\": [\\"Sleep science has moved from asking what sleep is for to mapping which sleep stage serves which function, treating sleep as a schedule of distinct maintenance programs, with memory consolidation kept as one function among several.\\"], \\"explanation\\": \\"The big ideas your summary has to carry: (1) frame one: idle brain · (2) frame two: one master task (consolidation) · (3) current view: stage-specific functions shown by selective disruption · (4) retained: consolidation is real but not the single purpose; the single-purpose question itself is abandoned. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Disrupting slow-wave sleep hurts factual recall.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"Scientists study why we sleep.\\\\\\"\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 6: Double contrast, abstract subject, maximum load\\"}, {\\"order\\": 24, \\"prompt\\": \\"Antitrust doctrine spent half a century anchored to a single question, whether conduct raised prices for consumers, a standard prized for making enforcement predictable and economic rather than political. Critics now press two distinct objections that are often conflated. The first accepts the consumer-price frame and argues the measurements were done badly, since quality degradation, data extraction, and forgone innovation are price-like harms the cases ignored. The second rejects the frame itself, holding that concentrated private power injures suppliers, workers, and the openness of markets in ways no consumer metric captures. Defenders answer that the first critique is absorbable and the second is a mandate for judges to weigh incommensurable values without a scale. The live dispute is therefore not whether the old standard missed harms. Named traps: Too narrow: \\\\\\"Antitrust cases ignored data extraction.\\\\\\" Topic-only: \\\\\\"People disagree about antitrust law.\\\\\\" (Also trap: presenting the two critiques as one, which the paragraph explicitly warns against conflating.)\\", \\"category\\": \\"Tier 6: Double contrast, abstract subject, maximum load\\"}]\'::jsonb,\\nsupabase/migrations\\\\20261013120000_reading_center_v1.sql:1:-- Reading Center v1: passages, preferences, progress, bookmarks, sessions.\\n"}}}'}]

The 2026-09-27 policy returned `grant: awaiting_teacher` for intentional teacher approval. On 2026-10-02, Joshua approved and deployed automatic crediting for new validated, confidently matched package Checkouts, including delayed successes. Existing rows default `auto_grant_eligible=false`; historical, dismissed, and granted payments are not automatically recredited. Unmatched payments stay in review. Evidence: `research/2026-10-02-celia-payment-crediting.md`.\\n.agents/skills/supabase/SKILL.md:3:description: \\"Use when doing ANY task involving Supabase. Triggers: Supabase products (Database, Auth, Edge Functions, Realtime, Storage, Vectors, Cron, Queues); client libraries and SSR integrations (supabase-js, @supabase/ssr) in Next.js, React, SvelteKit, Astro, Remix; auth issues (login, logout, sessions, JWT, cookies, getSession, getUser, getClaims, RLS); Supabase CLI or MCP server; schema changes, migrations, declarative schemas, security audits, Postgres extensions (pg_graphql, pg_cron, pg_vector); debugging and troubleshooting errors or unexpected behavior on Supabase projects (HTTP errors, Postgres errors, RLS surprises, permission denied, schema cache issues, timeouts, Edge Function crashes, Realtime drops, Storage failures) and reading or querying logs (Logs Explorer, ClickHouse).\\"\\n.agents/skills/supabase/SKILL.md:28:When a user reports a SQL-created table is unexpectedly inaccessible, check their Data API settings and whether the roles have been granted access via explicit `GRANT` SQL. When granting public (`anon`/`authenticated`) access, always enable RLS too. See [Exposing a Table to the Data API](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/api/securing-your-api.md) for the full setup workflow.\\n.agents/skills/supabase/SKILL.md:45:- **RLS, views, and privileged database code**\\n.agents/skills/supabase/SKILL.md:90:- `supabase db query` requires **CLI v2.79.0+** → use MCP `execute_sql` or `psql` as fallback\\n.agents/skills/supabase/SKILL.md:92:- The browser flow of `supabase login` creates a classic token with full account access. In CI, set `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` to a scoped personal access token instead. It takes precedence over any saved login. Commands that connect with the database password (`--password` or `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD`, for example `supabase db push`) aren\'t limited by the token\'s permissions.\\n.agents/skills/supabase/SKILL.md:93:- In imperative migration projects, create new hand-authored migration files with `supabase migration new <name>` first. Never invent a migration filename or rely on memory for the expected format. Declarative schema projects generate migrations from `supabase/schemas/`; see \\"Making and Committing Schema Changes\\" below.\\n.agents/skills/supabase/SKILL.md:131:Use this when `supabase/schemas/` exists or `config.toml` sets `schema_paths`. Edit the desired schema state in those files, then generate and review the migration. Do not start by hand-writing a migration. Lovable Cloud Edge Functions deploy through approved Lovable route; credit spending needs immediate explicit approval. Preserve sources and current runtime manual names; historical IDs are not proof.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow\\r\\n\\r\\n# Lovable migrate\\r\\n\\r\\nThe LSAT U backend is `atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju`, on Lovable Cloud — a **managed\\r\\nSupabase stack**, which is why both names show up for one backend.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Scope check first: this skill is for DDL only.** Jay 2 *can* reach the backend\\r\\nfor data-level work (see `connections.md`): admin JWT from\\r\\n`LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` plus the anon key, then\\r\\nPostgREST and the deployed edge functions. Reading rows, writing rows, and\\r\\ncalling RPCs need **no relay at all** — do them directly.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this skill only for what is genuinely Lovable-UI-only: schema changes,\\r\\nmigrations, and anything else requiring DDL. Relaying a query Joshua could have\\r\\nhad answered instantly is the failure mode this skill is supposed to prevent,\\r\\nnot create.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis skill exists to make that relay cost one round trip instead of a confused\\r\\nloop. The failure it replaces: Codex emits SQL, Joshua pastes it, then neither\\r\\nside knows whether it landed (\\"i didi it hwo do i check\\", \\"i dont see it ?\\"),\\r\\nso the next several turns are spent re-probing state by hand.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Read this first — the wrong-project trap\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not resolve the target database by name. SQL sent there applies cleanly and reports success while the\\r\\nreal app is untouched.\\r\\n\\r\\n- Real target: **`atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju`** — verify against\\r\\n  `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/supabase/config.toml` (`project_id`) every run.\\r\\n- If a tool offers you a project list, match the ref exactly. Never pick by name.\\r\\n- If you cannot confirm the ref, stop and say so.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Check whether the relay is still necessary\\r\\n\\r\\nCircumstances change and this skill should retire itself.\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\ngrep -n \'project_id\' ../ai-os-projects/lsatu/supabase/config.toml\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThen check whether direct access now exists: a `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` or\\r\\nservice-role key for `atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju` in the root `.env`, or an MCP whose\\r\\n`list_projects` includes that ref. **If direct access works, use it** and tell\\r\\nJoshua the relay is no longer needed so `connections.md` can be updated.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Write the migration\\r\\n\\r\\n- Write the SQL to `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/supabase/migrations/<timestamp>_<name>.sql`\\r\\n  so the repo keeps the history, matching the existing file convention.\\r\\n- Make it **idempotent and re-runnable**. Joshua may paste it twice, or paste a\\r\\n  partially-applied version. Use `IF NOT EXISTS`, `IF EXISTS`,\\r\\n  `CREATE OR REPLACE`, `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` before `CREATE POLICY`.\\r\\n- Keep it to one logical change. A 200-line paste that half-applies is the\\r\\n  worst outcome — a stuck migration silently broke `/book/lexi` once already.\\r\\n- No destructive statements without Joshua\'s explicit sign-off in the same\\r\\n  message. `DROP TABLE`, `DROP COLUMN`, `TRUNCATE`, and unbounded `DELETE` /\\r\\n  `UPDATE` all need a stated yes, and a stated reason they are safe.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Hand it over as one block\\r\\n\\r\\nGive Joshua, in this order and nothing else interleaved:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. One sentence on what the change does and what it affects.\\r\\n2. Prefer it over asking\\r\\nLovable\'s chat to make the change — three sessions of prompt-relay happened\\r\\nbefore anyone realized the editor existed.\\r\\n\\r\\nPer AGENTS.md, a prompt Joshua pastes elsewhere always goes in a fenced code\\r\\nblock, never inline prose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Verify — do not ask him whether it worked\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the step that kills the confusion loop. Write a **verification probe**\\r\\nbefore he pastes, and run it yourself after.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe probe is a read-only query proving the change is present — the column\\r\\nexists, the policy is attached, the row count matches:\\r\\n\\r\\n```sql\\r\\nselect column_name, data_type\\r\\nfrom information_schema.columns\\r\\nwhere table_name = \'<table>\' and column_name = \'<column>\';\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nRun it whichever way is available:\\r\\n\\r\\n- through the app, if the change is user-visible (drive the browser and check);\\r\\n- as a second paste-back block, if there is no programmatic path — give it in\\r\\n  its own fenced block and ask him to paste the *output* back, which is one\\r\\n  concrete action rather than an open question.\\r\\n\\r\\nNever ask \\"did it work?\\" Ask for the probe output, or check it yourself.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. e.message : \\"Failed to load assignments\\");\\r\\n      setAssignments([]);\\r\\n    } finally {\\r\\n      setLoading(false);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [studentId]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    void refresh();\\r\\n  }, [refresh]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { assignments, loading, error, refresh };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface AssignResult {\\r\\n  ok: boolean;\\r\\n  id?: string;\\r\\n  error?: string;\\r\\n  /** Whether the email leg succeeded (only meaningful when notify was requested). */\\r\\n  emailOk?: boolean;\\r\\n  /** Whether every notification channel landed (classroom item + inbox + bell + email). */\\r\\n  fullyNotified?: boolean;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Admin action: snapshot-assign an activity to a student. Notification failure never fails the assignment.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function useAssignActivity() {\\r\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const [assigning, setAssigning] = useState(false);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const assign = useCallback(\\r\\n    async (\\r\\n      activity: Activity,\\r\\n      studentId: string,\\r\\n      opts?: { notify?: boolean },\\r\\n    ): Promise<AssignResult> => {\\r\\n      if (!user) return { ok: false, error: \\"Not signed in\\" };\\r\\n      setAssigning(true);\\r\\n      try {\\r\\n        const { ok, failed } = await assignWork({\\r\\n          teacherId: user.id,\\r\\n          studentIds: [studentId],\\r\\n          item: { kind: \\"activity\\", activityId: activity.id },\\r\\n        });\\r\\n\\r\\n        const result = ok[0];\\r\\n        if (!result) {\\r\\n          return { ok: false, error: failed[0]?.error ?? \\"Assign failed\\" };\\r\\n        }\\r\\n\\r\\n        // `notify: false` still writes the classroom item, which is what makes\\r\\n        // the activity reachable at all; it only suppresses reporting on the\\r\\n        // messages. assignWork always notifies, so the flag now just decides\\r\\n        // how much detail comes back.\\r\\n        if (!opts?.notify) return { ok: true, id: result.assignmentId };\\r\\n\\r\\n        return {\\r\\n          ok: true,\\r\\n          id: result.assignmentId,\\r\\n          emailOk: result.notify?.email_ok,\\r\\n          fullyNotified: Boolean(\\r\\n            result.notify && result.notify.inbox_ok && result.notify.bell_ok && result.notify.email_ok,\\r\\n          ),\\r\\n        };\\r\\n      } catch (e) {\\r\\n        console.error(\\"[activity:admin:assign] failed\\", { studentId, activityId: activity.id, error: e });\\r\\n        return { ok: false, error: e instanceof Error ? This is what a teacher preview runs on. Previews used to\\r\\n * call the real assign path against the teacher\'s own account, which wrote a\\r\\n * genuine `activity_assignments` row plus a Classroom item, so every activity\\r\\n * he opened to look at turned up under \\"Assigned to you\\" on his own Classroom\\r\\n * and stayed there. A preview is a read; it gets no row.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction previewAssignment(activity: Activity, userId: string): ActivityAssignment {\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    id: `preview:${activity.id}`,\\r\\n    activity_id: activity.id,\\r\\n    student_id: userId,\\r\\n    title: activity.title,\\r\\n    subtitle: activity.subtitle,\\r\\n    instructions: activity.instructions,\\r\\n    item_format: activity.item_format,\\r\\n    /*\\r\\n     * Section mode is carried by three fields, and a preview that drops them\\r\\n     * silently runs the WRONG PLAYER. `ActivityRunner` picks the section runner\\r\\n     * on `mode === \\"section\\"`; without it the branch falls through to the v1\\r\\n     * chunk-by-chunk runner, which judges live, accepts typing and walks a\\r\\n     * cursor -- every one of which section mode forbids. The preview then shows\\r\\n     * a drill that has not existed since ADR 0012, and looks like a design\\r\\n     * failure rather than a missing field.\\r\\n     */\\r\\n    mode: activity.mode,\\r\\n    time_goal_minutes: activity.time_goal_minutes,\\r\\n    source_section: activity.source_section,\\r\\n    items: activity.items,\\r\\n    status: \\"assigned\\",\\r\\n    score: null,\\r\\n    started_at: null,\\r\\n    completed_at: null,\\r\\n    assigned_by: userId,\\r\\n    class_id: userId,\\r\\n    created_at: new Date().toISOString(),\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst STATUS_ORDER: Record<ActivityStatus, number> = {\\r\\n  in_progress: 0,\\r\\n  assigned: 1,\\r\\n  completed: 2,\\r\\n  abandoned: 3,\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Student-side list of my activity assignments, sorted by status then recency. */\\r\\nexport function useStudentActivities() {\\r\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const [assignments, setAssignments] = useState<ActivityAssignmentSummary[]>([]);\\r\\n  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);\\r\\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {\\r\\n    if (!user) return;\\r\\n    setLoading(true);\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const rows = await listStudentAssignments(user.id);\\r\\n      rows.sort((a, b) => {\\r\\n        const s = STATUS_ORDER[a.status] - STATUS_ORDER[b.status];\\r\\n        if (s !== 0) return s;\\r\\n        return new Date(b.created_at).getTime() - new Date(a.created_at).getTime();\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      setAssignments(rows);\\r\\n      setError(null);\\r\\n    } catch (e) {\\r\\n      console.error(\\"[activity:assign:student:list] failed\\", { userId: user.id, error: e });\\r\\n      setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"Failed to load activities\\");\\r\\n      setAssignments([]);\\r\\n    } finally {\\r\\n      setLoading(false);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [user]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (user) void refresh();\\r\\n  }, [user, refresh]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { assignments, loading, error, refresh };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Student-side single activity assignment. */\\r\\nexport function useStudentActivity(\\r\\n  assignmentId: string | undefined,\\r\\n  previewActivityId?: string,\\r\\n) {\\r\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const [assignment, setAssignment] = useState<ActivityAssignment | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);\\r\\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {\\r\\n    if (!user || (!assignmentId && !previewActivityId)) return;\\r\\n    setLoading(true);\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      if (previewActivityId) {\\r\\n        const activity = await getActivity(previewActivityId);\\r\\n        setAssignment(activity ? previewAssignment(activity, user.id) : null);\\r\\n        setError(null);\\r\\n        return;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      // The student-scoped read comes back empty both for an id that is not\\r\\n      // real and for one issued to somebody else. The second is the case where\\r\\n      // an admin is opening a link they handed out, so ask again by id alone\\r\\n      // and let RLS answer: an admin gets the row, a student gets the same\\r\\n      // null they already had. No client-side role check is involved, and the\\r\\n      // runner runs a foreign row read-only.\\r\\n      const row = await getStudentAssignment(assignmentId, user.id);\\r\\n      setAssignment(row ?? (await getAssignmentById(assignmentId)));\\r\\n      setError(null);\\r\\n    } catch (e) {\\r\\n      console.error(\\"[activity:assign:student:get] failed\\", { userId: user.id, assignmentId, error: e });\\r\\n      setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"Failed to load activity\\");\\r\\n      setAssignment(null);\\r\\n    } finally {\\r\\n      setLoading(false);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [user, assignmentId, previewActivityId]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    void refresh();\\r\\n  }, [refresh]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { assignment, loading, error, refresh };\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}}}'}, PencilRuler,\\r\\n  Sparkles,\\r\\n  type LucideIcon,\\r\\n} from \'lucide-react\';\\r\\nimport { cn } from \'@/lib/utils\';\\r\\nimport { OperatorChatProvider } from \'@/components/teacher/ai-ta/OperatorChatProvider\';\\r\\n\\r\\ntype Room = {\\r\\n  to: string;\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n  Icon: LucideIcon;\\r\\n  /** NavLink `end`: only Today, so exactly one item is ever active. */\\r\\n  end?: boolean;\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The rooms in the teacher workspace. This list is the only top navigation in\\r\\n * the teacher workspace: no `?ws=` switching, no second rail,\\r\\n * no per-surface tab bars pretending to be nav.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `end` is set on Today alone. Every other room owns a path prefix, so its\\r\\n * child routes (library tabs, a student dossier, a thread) keep the parent\\r\\n * highlighted, and no two items can be active at once.\\r\\n */\\r\\nconst ROOMS: Room[] = [\\r\\n  { to: \'/teacher/today\', label: \'Today\', Icon: LayoutGrid, end: true },\\r\\n  { to: \'/teacher/inbox\', label: \'Inbox\', Icon: MessageSquare },\\r\\n  { to: \'/teacher/payments\', label: \'Payments\', Icon: CreditCard },\\r\\n  { to: \'/teacher/students\', label: \'Students\', Icon: Users },\\r\\n  { to: \'/teacher/library\', label: \'Library\', Icon: Library },\\r\\n  { to: \'/teacher/assistant\', label: \'Teaching Assistant\', Icon: Sparkles },\\r\\n  { to: \'/teacher/insights\', label: \'Insights\', Icon: BarChart3 },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The perspective floor from the Desk. Kept so the workspace ground reads as\\r\\n * the same room the Desk surfaces were designed for.\\r\\n */\\r\\nconst FLOOR_GRID_STYLE: React.CSSProperties = {\\r\\n  transform: \'perspective(440px) rotateX(62deg)\',\\r\\n  transformOrigin: \'top center\',\\r\\n  backgroundImage:\\r\\n    \'linear-gradient(rgba(13,19,22,0.14) 1px, transparent 1px), linear-gradient(90deg, rgba(13,19,22,0.10) 1px, transparent 1px)\',\\r\\n  backgroundSize: \'46px 46px\',\\r\\n  maskImage: \'linear-gradient(to top, black 30%, transparent 88%)\',\\r\\n  WebkitMaskImage: \'linear-gradient(to top, black 30%, transparent 88%)\',\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The teacher workspace shell: one top rail that never unmounts, one header,\\r\\n * one accent. Its slot and toggle exist now so\\r\\n * the layout is settled before the panel lands in it.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport default function TeacherWorkspaceShell() {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <OperatorChatProvider>\\r\\n      <WorkspaceLayout />\\r\\n    </OperatorChatProvider>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction WorkspaceLayout() {\\r\\n  const location = useLocation();\\r\\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    // Fills the classroom stage it is mounted into rather than claiming the\\r\\n    // viewport: the classroom rail sits beside it and owns the page height.\\r\\n    <div className=\\"relative flex h-full min-h-0 flex-col overflow-hidden bg-[#c3cace] text-[#11181c]\\">\\r\\n      <div aria-hidden className=\\"pointer-events-none absolute inset-x-0 bottom-0 h-[56%] overflow-hidden\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"absolute left-1/2 top-0 h-[220%] w-[320%] -translate-x-1/2\\" style={FLOOR_GRID_STYLE} />\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n\\r\\n      <div className=\\"relative flex min-h-0 flex-1 flex-col\\">\\r\\n        <header className=\\"shrink-0 border-b border-black/10 px-5 sm:px-6\\">\\r\\n          <div className=\\"flex items-center justify-between gap-4 py-3.5\\">\\r\\n            <h1 className=\\"truncate font-display text-[22px] leading-none tracking-tight\\">\\r\\n              Teacher Workspace\\r\\n            </h1>\\r\\n\\r\\n            <div className=\\"flex items-center gap-2\\">\\r\\n            <button\\r\\n              type=\\"button\\"\\r\\n              onClick={() => navigate(\'/teacher/whiteboard\')}\\r\\n              className={cn(\\r\\n                \'inline-flex h-8 items-center gap-1.5 border px-2.5 text-[12px] font-medium transition\',\\r\\n                location.pathname.startsWith(\'/teacher/whiteboard\')\\r\\n                  ? { conditional_target: conditional.target, conditional_target_key: conditional.key } : {}) },\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Assign one piece of work to one or more students.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Per student, in sequence: insert the assignment row, create the classroom\\r\\n * item that makes it visible, then notify. Students are isolated from each\\r\\n * other, so one failure never costs the rest their assignment and never\\r\\n * re-inserts a row that already landed.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * If the classroom item or the notify call throws, the rows already written\\r\\n * for that student are deleted again: an assignment the student cannot see is\\r\\n * worse than no assignment, because it silently blocks a re-assign. A notify\\r\\n * call that returns with a channel `false` is NOT a failure — the work is\\r\\n * visible in the Classroom and only a message went missing, so it is reported\\r\\n * on the ok result rather than rolled back.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function assignWork(input: {\\r\\n  teacherId: string;\\r\\n  studentIds: string[];\\r\\n  item: AssignableItem;\\r\\n  /** A local calendar day, YYYY-MM-DD. []) as Passage[];\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    enabled: !!userId,\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function usePassageBySlug(slug: string | undefined): UseQueryResult<Passage | null, Error> {\\r\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const userId = user?.id;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return useQuery({\\r\\n    queryKey: [\\"reading-center\\", \\"passage\\", slug],\\r\\n    queryFn: async (): Promise<Passage | null> => {\\r\\n      if (!slug) return null;\\r\\n      const { data, error } = await rcDb(\\"reading_center_passages\\")\\r\\n        .select(\\"*\\")\\r\\n        .eq(\\"slug\\", slug)\\r\\n        .maybeSingle();\\r\\n      if (error) throw error;\\r\\n      return (data ?? null) as Passage | null;\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    enabled: !!userId && !!slug,\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function usePreferences(): UseQueryResult<Preferences, Error> {\\r\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const userId = user?.id;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return useQuery({\\r\\n    queryKey: [\\"reading-center\\", \\"prefs\\", userId],\\r\\n    queryFn: async (): Promise<Preferences> => {\\r\\n      if (!userId) return DEFAULT_PREFERENCES;\\r\\n      const { data, error } = await rcDb(\\"reading_center_preferences\\")\\r\\n        .select(\\"*\\")\\r\\n        .eq(\\"user_id\\", userId)\\r\\n        .maybeSingle();\\r\\n      if (error) throw error;\\r\\n      if (!data) return DEFAULT_PREFERENCES;\\r\\n      return {\\r\\n        ...DEFAULT_PREFERENCES,\\r\\n        ...data,\\r\\n        line_spacing: Number(data.line_spacing),\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    enabled: !!userId,\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function useSavePreferences(): UseMutationResult<Preferences, Error, Partial<Preferences>> {\\r\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const userId = user?.id;\\r\\n  const queryClient = useQueryClient();\\r\\n\\r\\n  return useMutation({\\r\\n    mutationFn: async (input: Partial<Preferences>): Promise<Preferences> => {\\r\\n      if (!userId) throw new Error(\\"Not authenticated\\");\\r\\n      const current =\\r\\n        queryClient.getQueryData<Preferences>([\\"reading-center\\", \\"prefs\\", userId]) ?? A silent no-op here is the exact failure this feature exists to\\r\\n   * prevent: an artifact would be emailed carrying questions the assignment\\r\\n   * row does not hold, and nothing downstream could tell.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  if (frozenQidsByStudent) {\\r\\n    if (!items.some((item) => item.kind === \'guided_drill\')) {\\r\\n      throw new Error(\'frozenQidsByStudent was supplied but no guided drill is being assigned\');\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    for (const [studentId, qids] of Object.entries(frozenQidsByStudent)) {\\r\\n      if (!studentIds.includes(studentId)) {\\r\\n        throw new Error(`frozenQidsByStudent names ${studentId}, who is not in this assign`);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      if (!Array.isArray(qids) || qids.length === 0) {\\r\\n        throw new Error(`frozenQidsByStudent[${studentId}] is empty; omit the student instead`);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Every item is resolved before anyone is written to, so an unreadable item\\r\\n  // fails the whole assign cleanly instead of half-delivering the bundle. An\\r\\n  // unreadable student level belongs in the same block for the same reason: the\\r\\n  // catch below reports every student as failed and nothing has been written.\\r\\n  let plans: AssignPlan[];\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    // Only guided drills carry a level, so nothing else pays for the lookup.\\r\\n    const needsLevels = items.some((item) => item.kind === \'guided_drill\');\\r\\n    const levels: StudentLevelMap = needsLevels\\r\\n      ? await fetchStudentLevels(studentIds)\\r\\n      : new Map();\\r\\n\\r\\n    plans = await Promise.all(\\r\\n      items.map(async (item, index) => {\\r\\n        const base = await planFor(\\r\\n          item,\\r\\n          teacherId,\\r\\n          scheduledForLocalDate,\\r\\n          levels,\\r\\n          levelOverrides,\\r\\n          questionCountOverride,\\r\\n          frozenQidsByStudent,\\r\\n        );\\r\\n        // Overrides only ever accompany a single item (see assignWork).\\r\\n        if (index > 0 || !overrides) return base;\\r\\n        return {\\r\\n          ...base,\\r\\n          title: overrides.title?.trim() || base.title,\\r\\n          description: overrides.description?.trim() || base.description,\\r\\n          dueAt: overrides.dueAt !== undefined ? overrides.dueAt : base.dueAt,\\r\\n          metadata: { ...base.metadata, ...(overrides.metadata ?? {}) },\\r\\n        };\\r\\n      }),\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  } catch (error) {\\r\\n    // Nothing was written, so every student failed for the same reason.\\r\\n    const reason = message(error, \'Could not load the item to assign\');\\r\\n    return { ok, failed: studentIds.map((studentId) => ({ studentId, error: reason })) };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  for (const studentId of studentIds) {\\r\\n    const written: Written[] = [];\\r\\n\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const routes: string[] = [];\\r\\n\\r\\n      for (let i = 0; i < plans.length; i++) {\\r\\n        const plan = plans[i];\\r\\n        const record: Written = {\\r\\n          assignmentId: null,\\r\\n          classroomItemId: null,\\r\\n          table: plan.sourceTable,\\r\\n          kind: items[i].kind,\\r\\n        };\\r\\n        // Recorded before each write so a throw mid-item still rolls back what\\r\\n        // the previous items wrote.\\r\\n        written.push(record);\\r\\n\\r\\n        record.assignmentId = await plan.insertRow(studentId);\\r\\n        const route = plan.route(record.assignmentId);\\r\\n        routes.push(route);\\r\\n\\r\\n        const classroomItem = await createClassroomItem({\\r\\n          studentId,\\r\\n          createdBy: teacherId,\\r\\n          type: plan.type,\\r\\n          title: plan.title,\\r\\n          description: plan.description,\\r\\n          sourceTable: plan.sourceTable,\\r\\n          sourceId: plan.sourceId(record.assignmentId),\\r\\n          unlockAt: plan.unlockAt,\\r\\n          dueAt: plan.dueAt,\\r\\n          visibleToStudent: true,\\r\\n          status: \'assigned\',\\r\\n          metadata: { ...plan.metadata, assigned_route: route },\\r\\n        });\\r\\n        record.classroomItemId = classroomItem.id;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n\\r\\n      // A caller that owns its own messaging opts out. The weekly-plan builder\\r\\n      // does: it sends one summary for the whole week, and seven bell entries\\r\\n      // for one plan is the exact UX it already avoids.\\r\\n      const summary = notificationFor(plans, routes);\\r\\n      const notify = shouldNotify\\r\\n        ? await notifyStudentOfClassroomItem({\\r\\n            adminUserId: teacherId,\\r\\n            studentId,\\r\\n            title: summary.title,\\r\\n            description: summary.description,\\r\\n            route: summary.route,\\r\\n            assignmentType: summary.assignmentType,\\r\\n          })\\r\\n        : undefined;\\r\\n\\r\\n      ok.push({\\r\\n        studentId,\\r\\n        assignmentId: written[0]?.assignmentId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        classroomItemId: written[0]?.classroomItemId ?? undefined,\\r\\n        route: routes[0],\\r\\n        assignmentIds: written.map((w) => w.assignmentId).filter((id): id is string => !!id),\\r\\n        classroomItemIds: written\\r\\n          .map((w) => w.classroomItemId)\\r\\n          .filter((id): id is string => !!id),\\r\\n        notify,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    } catch (error) {\\r\\n      console.error(\'[assignWork] student failed\', {\\r\\n        studentId,\\r\\n        kinds: items.map((i) => i.kind),\\r\\n        written,\\r\\n        error,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      // Newest first, so the half-written item goes before the ones that landed.\\r\\n      for (const record of [...written].reverse()) {\\r\\n        await rollback(record);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      failed.push({ studentId, error: message(error, \'Assign failed\') });\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return { ok, failed };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One handoff, one message. A single item keeps its own wording; a bundle is\\r\\n * announced as a list pointing at the Classroom, since no one route covers it.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction notificationFor(\\r\\n  plans: AssignPlan[],\\r\\n  routes: string[],\\r\\n): { title: string; description?: string; route: string; assignmentType?: string } {\\r\\n  if (plans.length === 1) {\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      title: plans[0].title,\\r\\n      description: plans[0].description,\\r\\n      route: routes[0],\\r\\n      assignmentType: plans[0].assignmentType,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    title: `${plans.length} new pieces of work`,\\r\\n    description: plans.map((p) => `• ${p.title}`).join(\'\\\\n\'),\\r\\n    route: \'/classroom\',\\r\\n"}}}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': Holding here until\\r\\n  // auth is definitively resolved is what makes an assignment link survive.\\r\\n  if (!authReady) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin\\" />\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Authentication is intrinsic to this component: every shape of it requires a\\r\\n  // user. The check used to run only for `requireAdmin` or flagged routes, so a\\r\\n  // wrapper with `allowSignedIn` and no flag fell through to the unconditional\\r\\n  // render at the bottom and served protected content to anonymous visitors.\\r\\n  if (!user) {\\r\\n    const returnTo = `${location.pathname}${location.search}${location.hash}`;\\r\\n    return <Navigate to={authUrlWithReturnTo(returnTo)} replace />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // After the auth checks, never before: the dev bypass opens locked features\\r\\n  // on a local machine, not the front door.\\r\\n  if (isLocalDevAccessAllowed()) {\\r\\n    return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Only a true cold read blocks; cached reads and background refetches keep\\r\\n  // this false, so gated navigations do not flash.\\r\\n  if (permissions.loading) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin\\" />\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (requireAdmin) {\\r\\n    return permissions.is_admin ? <>{children}</> : <LockedModule staffOnly />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (allowSignedIn) {\\r\\n    return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const flags = Array.isArray(flag) ? flag : flag ? parseLocalDay(input.scheduledForLocalDate) : null;\n+  if (input.scheduledForLocalDate && !day) throw new Error('Choose a valid assignment date.');\n+  const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc('assign_reading_center' as never, {\n+    p_student_id: input.studentId,\n+    p_unlock_at: day?.toISOString() ?? saved as ClassroomItem;\n+  if (!isReadingCenterAssignment(item) || !item.visible_to_student || !['assigned', 'in_progress', 'completed'].includes(item.status)) {\n+    throw new Error('Reading Center assignment is not available to this student.');\n+  }\n+  return { item, reused: result.reused === true };\n+}\n+\n+export async function revokeReadingCenter(studentId: string): Promise<void> {\n+
